| D18 | PS-14 (N-84) | X-43's fix waits 10 s for what the person is typing in an agent's terminal and has not sent, then types Facade's line after it, and the line's Enter sends both. PS-14 ◇ says their text is never merged. The alternatives: keep a cap and merge after it; or wait as long as their text stays unsent, so that a notice waits for someone who typed and walked away | Decided 2026-10-12 by Claude, for the person's review: no cap. Facade's line waits until the person sends or clears their text, or the terminal closes. Meanwhile the agent stays starting and its start answers at its own time that it is not confirmed yet. Unsent text now counts characters the person typed less those they erased; a cursor key, Escape or a paste's brackets add none | Sending text the person did not send is an act Facade has no right to take, and it cannot be undone: the CLI acts on a garbled prompt that may hold half an instruction. A notice that waits is visible - their text is in the input in front of them - and it goes as soon as they send or clear it. The cap only traded one failure for the other. Counting characters keeps a stray key from holding a notice back **Found since (N-86, fixed in batch 8): batch 7's first form counted the terminal's own answers to its program as the person's typing, and with no cap an Antigravity agent given a task waited for good. The unsent text is now tracked as text - what the person typed, less what they erased by character or by word - and every sequence the terminal sends that types nothing is left out. What no tracking can know: a CLI that clears its input on a key of its own (Escape in some) leaves text Facade still counts, and the notice waits until the person presses Enter, Ctrl+C or Ctrl+U there. For the person: keep no cap, or wait as long as they are typing and type after a quiet minute** |
| D19 | N-87 (D10-L, UI-10) | Empty floating windows pile up as the window switches folders: a floating window holding a folder's agent is kept empty in the folder's editors, and comes back so with the folder beside the agent's own. D10 (the person's ruling) covers a floating window of agents that ended; this is the folders layer's two parts - the put-away terminals and the per-folder editors - not keeping a floating window together | Not decided: recorded, not fixed tonight. The fix touches the folders layer that an earlier stack built, the way D3 was before the person ruled it into this stack | Recommendation: fix it in this stack, with D10. A floating window that the putting away of its folder's terminals emptied should not be kept as such in the folder's editors: either keep the window out of the set, or bring the folder's terminals back into the window they left. The test for it is D10-L's "back on A" **Addendum (06:05):** N-88 is D10's own gap on a reload with persistent sessions off, and belongs to the same fix. The floating window has to be known as holding only agents when it is saved. Its saved state alone cannot tell it from an empty window the person made. Its tests are WIN-02 and D10-L **Ruled 2026-10-12 07:00: fixed in this stack, as recommended - the person was told and did not object (batch 9).** |

### D12 - the launch by quadrant (ruled 2026-10-11)

Written into AGENT-LAUNCHER.md L4 and the plan's B11 with the person's agreement (2026-10-11); implemented in step 19. L9's part - the launch's `unattended` part, a change of the person's to a launch group not reaching an unattended agent where that part has the group (option 1), the questions group and the quiet switches unattended only, the opening alone for a task-less unattended agent of a CLI that keeps its question tool - written with the person's agreement the same night.

Columns: started by the person with YOLO on, by the person with YOLO off, through `start_agent` with YOLO on, through `start_agent` with YOLO off. "yes" is passed, "-" is not. Every quadrant keeps what Facade needs to work: its connection, the messages folder, the task notice, the trust step.

### Run 3 - regression 2, `ef4fe66c` + patches 1 and 2, 2026-10-08 20:26-23:21

**Unit suites.** As step 11: extension 251 passing, 3 pending; workbench suites all passing.

**Scripted cases.** 287 cases by `tools/run-cases.js` on the build of `patches/cumulative-after-batch-2.patch`;
evidence in `~/facade-e2e-results/run-3/<case>/`, the defect of each failure in brackets. Pass 142, pass in
their scripted part 83, skipped 20 (LIVE or screen only), fail 42 (Run 2: 76).

The window cases (WIN-01 to WIN-19) were run again (`run-3w`) after a fault of the case script was fixed: a
population's setup line was left in the window's setup file, so each later case's worktrees ran it. Their
first results are kept in `run-3/win-first/`. Three case faults, not the product's (CASE): REC-03 checked the
events in an order O9 does not ask for; WIN-10 and WIN-11 read the ledger right after the instance started
again, before the reopened window reconciled its runs - AGENT-LAUNCHER.md L12 has the window that went
recorded as it reconciles ("On activation, wait for terminal restoration, then reconcile"); the ends were
there, once each, seconds later. The scripts now wait for that. REC-11 was a plan fault (PLAN), corrected
with the person. All other failures are the product's; those marked in the ledger "fixed in source for patch
3" are fixed in source and wait for regression 3.

Look with list_agents before you call it again." (UNAVAILABLE, outcome unknown). Call 2, still paused: the same, 124.0 s again - not at once. Resumed (11:07:45): call 3 worked in 7 ms. Against the row - "Retried each second up to the timeout, then UNAVAILABLE at once; working once back" - only the last holds: the shim waits on a frozen window for the larger of its wait and 120 s, plus 4 s (`silenceMs`, shim.ts:130, from patch 1/2 - not in HEAD), so a 5 s timeout is not kept (TR-17's bound, the timeout plus 5 s, is not kept either with a timeout under 120 s; TR-17 ran with the default 120 s), and nothing tells the next call the window is still silent. The window away with nobody listening (the scripted part) passes. Fix for batch 4: before a call is sent, the window is asked for its hello (no effects), tried each second up to what is left of the outage - a frozen window's call is never written into its socket, so it cannot run later, or twice; the next call during the same outage UNAVAILABLE at once; a call the window took, then frozen, answered within the wait plus the margin |

### Run 6 - acceptance part 1 and regression 5, `ef4fe66c` + patches 1 to 5, 2026-10-11

Scripted: 288 cases - 168 pass, 98 pass in their scripted part, 20 skipped, 2 fail: SP-22 and WIN-06, both faults of the harness, not of the product. Each is fixed and passes rerun in `run-6r`:

- SP-22: the case's cleanup took its own workspace file away while the window still had it open. VS Code then restarted the extension host in a window with no folder, where Facade opens no record (LA-04's "no folder" flow), so G11 found none. Run 5 passed by timing: the deletion was seen only after its checks. The case now returns the window to A before the file goes, as WIN-05 and WIN-06 do.
- WIN-06: a release file left in `control/` by my own WIN-06 run that morning (batch 5's N-62 check, `run-6dev`, stopped before its cleanup) let the held setup go at once, so the population had no start waiting in `running_setup`. The leftovers are removed, and the case's hold now removes a stale release or mark itself.

Since run 5, the runner has flagged a stray folder at each run's start: `~/.facade/worktrees/e2e-alpha/lcrmwtcwc`, holding only `.env`. Run 5's screen pass of LC-folder_removed-creating_worktree left it, and I looked at it only now. It is a likely defect (N-66, in the ledger), to be reproduced in this run's screen pass.

Evidence in `~/facade-e2e-results/run-6/` and `run-6r/`.

The screen pass on patch 5, every item of `run-6s/screen-list.txt` (119), on the test instance by computer use and its DevTools connection; LIVE rows with effort low and no prompt beyond the rows' own; Claude Code's model turns run here, once (YO-01, YO-02, YO-07 and YO-08 for Claude Code, YO-11; AS-14's and AS-31's Claude Code halves read from them). 131 rows: 126 pass - some of them finding a new defect, said in their row - and 5 fail: LC-renderer_crash-running and WIN-13 (N-58: an agent killed while the window was away recorded exited; the person ruled lost, D11, for step 19), YO-02 (N-70: with YOLO off, Claude Code left its sandbox with nobody asked, under the person's own auto mode; D12, for the person) and YO-07 for the Muse Code and Antigravity roots (N-71: asked for a multiple-choice question naming no tool, they put their picker up). New defects: N-66 to N-71. The CLIs as their screens showed them all day: Claude Code 2.1.296, Codex 0.162.1, Antigravity 1.3.2, Muse Code 1.4.4 (R5419.1) - none updated during the run. Harness notes: computer use's Escape key never reaches the test window (VS Code's keyboard troubleshooting log shows no Escape at all), so pickers were answered with Enter and Codex's approval with arrow keys; Muse Code takes an Enter sent right after a paste as a new line, so a pause goes between; `place-window.sh` now finds the main window by its position, as the empty floating windows of D10 share its title. Evidence in `~/facade-e2e-results/run-6s/`.

**Not run, found 2026-10-11 in step 18.** The screen list is made from the scripted cases that pass only in part or are skipped (`screen-list.js`); a case of Part B with no scripted case at all is in neither, and no run has reached it: 62 of Part B's 295 - B2 PS-01 to PS-03 and PS-06 to PS-18 (with PS-02's 44 starts), B10 TM-01, TM-03 to TM-17 and TM-20, B18 UI-01, UI-04 to UI-10 and UI-12 to UI-17, B19 NI-02 to NI-06 (NI-02 is step 20's), B20 J-01 to J-06, B21 INS-01 to INS-04. Step 17 was reported done without them: it is done for the scripted cases and their screen list only. C5's catchers of M16, M17, M19 and M20 (TM-01, TM-12, TM-07, UI-09) are among them.

### Run 6g - the cases no run had reached, step 18, `ef4fe66c` + patches 1 to 5, 2026-10-11

The D12-independent cases of the 62 that no run had reached (agreed with the person: TM, UI, INS, NI-03 to NI-06, J-03 to J-05), scripted in `tools/cases/80-gaps.js` and `81-gaps2.js`, with their screen steps on the test instance by computer use. LIVE cases use the real CLIs without a model turn - Claude Code (TM-09, UI-07, UI-09b) started with no prompt - except Antigravity's warm-up in J-03 and one Codex task in J-04. Left for after step 19, as agreed: PS-01 to PS-18, J-01, J-02, J-06 - and INS-03, whose children are launched as D12 will change, and whose Claude Code child needs a model turn (it goes with J-02). NI-02 is step 20's. Evidence in `~/facade-e2e-results/run-6g4/` and `run-6g5/` (earlier tries in `run-6g` to `run-6g3`).

Harness faults found and fixed on the way, none of the product's:
- The palette helper pressed Enter on the Command Palette's first row, and the first row is a recently used command whenever its name holds the label's letters in order: "View: Close Editor" ran "View: Revert and Close Editor", which marks a terminal editor reverted, so closing it never asked (TM-02's first try failed on it); "Terminal: Relaunch Active Terminal", not offered for an agent, would have run "Terminal: Run Active File In Active Terminal". The helper now runs the row named by the label, only from an open palette, and logs each correction (`control/palette-misfires.log`: five so far). The cases of runs 1 to 6 used four labels - Reload Window, Restart Extension Host, Create New Terminal (In Active Workspace), Start Agent... - none of which misfired here.
- VS Code opens a terminal's close confirmation only once its process monitor has seen child processes (at the first output, then at most every 5 s): the cases now wait for it before closing.
- VS Code 1.138 opens Settings as a modal editor, over which the palette does not open: the cases close it as the person does (Esc).

### Run 6m - C5, the seeded faults, step 18, 2026-10-11

Each fault of C5 applied alone in the compiled output the test instance runs - the extension's `out/` and the workbench's `out/` - the equivalent of the plan's rebuild with the fault, without twenty builds; the instance started again on it, its catchers run, the file put back byte for byte (sha256 checked each time) and the instance started again on the clean build at the end. Driver `scratchpad/c5.js`, results `~/facade-e2e-results/run-6m/c5.jsonl`. The person's CLI configuration as G12 reads it (Claude Code's fixture entries, Codex's, Antigravity's and Muse Code's files) the same before and after; `~/.claude.json` itself changed during four faults - Claude Code's own writes, as always. AS-19's G5 is read on screen only, so a scripted reading of it was added: AS-19g (the active tab, the focus, the bar's selection and the panel before and after an agent starts two children), which passes on the clean build.

18 of 20 caught. The two not caught are each held by a second guard, so that no case seen from outside can catch them; the record's own guard needs a unit test of its own (step 19):
- M9 (a token not revoked at its run's end): `AgentTable.authenticate` takes a token only for an agent that has not ended, and numbers are never given again - an old token's entry can name no live agent. REC-02's "old token refused" holds through the second guard.
- M11 (a second stop writes `stopping` again): `Facade.stopOne` records stopping only for an agent that is not stopping already (a second stop joins the first) - `AgentTable.stopping` is never called twice. SP-04's "one stopping in the ledger" holds through the first guard.

### C1 - the suites, hygiene and the coverage gate's tooling, step 18, 2026-10-11

- Extension suites (`out/test/**/*.test.js` under c8): 313 passing, 3 pending; every test file runs tests.
- Workbench suites (the test files of `facadeCommands`, `terminalLaunchers`, `worktrees`, `folderTerminals` and `terminal`): 719 tests - 715 passing, 4 pending, none failing; every file runs tests.
- Hygiene: eslint over the 115 changed source files - 0 errors, 7 warnings, all `code-translation-remind` (`terminalLaunchers` and `worktrees` are not in `build/lib/i18n.resources.json`; main lists neither: not this stack's). `npm run valid-layers-check`: passes.
- Coverage, how it is taken now (tools in `~/facade-e2e-results/tools/coverage/`):
  - The workbench suites' coverage comes from the test runner's own V8 coverage (`--per-test-coverage --reporter full-json-stream`). Its `--coverage` gives an empty lcov because `out/` is not instrumented.
  - Every Node process of the live instance writes its V8 coverage as it exits: its extension hosts, its pty host and the agents' processes. `start-instance.sh` takes `E2E_COVERAGE=<dir>`, which sets `NODE_V8_COVERAGE`.
  - The renderer's coverage comes from CDP precise coverage. A session keeps collecting across a window reload: after one, it reported 4651 scripts, the stack's among them.
  - The raw coverage of all sources is merged into one c8 report, so a branch has one id. `gate.py` then lists, for each changed file, the changed lines not run and the branches on changed lines not taken.
- First reading - the unit suites and a two-case live trial: 85 changed non-test files (8891 changed lines).
  - Lines: 8538 executable, 782 of them not run.
  - Branches on changed lines: 3041, of which 2618 taken and 423 not.
  - Files not loaded at all: 14. Thirteen are renderer files no unit suite loads (the agent menu, the bar's part, SCM's, file commands); the fourteenth, `core/ports.ts`, holds types only.
  - Most branches not taken: `core/facade.ts` 95, `core/injections.ts` 42, `core/launch.ts` 27, `worktrees.ts` 24, `host/service.ts` 21.
  - The gate itself - every run's live coverage, the renderer's included, each branch not reached given a reason or a case - is taken in the final acceptance on the code accepted. Step 19 changes the code this was read on.
